## 1. Exclusive Control Of Quantum Memory Erasure

- **Score**: 8.8 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Res.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Research
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-10-20
- **Authors**: Mir Alimuddin, Nathan Shettell, Raja Yehia, Antonio Acín, And Federico Centrone
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/6jtz-g59p](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/6jtz-g59p)
- **Tags**: `quantum Landauer erasure`  `assisted quantum erasure`  `quantum memory`  `thermodynamic work`  `quantum correlations`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces and analyzes assisted quantum erasure, showing how correlations with a remote system can reduce the thermodynamic work cost of resetting a quantum memory beyond the standard Landauer bound.

### Motivation

Memory erasure is a fundamental quantum information task governed by Landauer's principle, which ties information loss to thermodynamic work, and the work asks whether external quantum correlations can alter this energetic cost.

### Method

The authors define assisted quantum erasure protocols in which a memory is erased while correlated with a remote system, and they quantify the energetic cost using information-theoretic and thermodynamic tools.

### Result

They find that correlations can reduce the work required for erasure, establishing an assisted erasure advantage and clarifying how remote quantum information influences Landauer-type energetic bounds.

### Conclusion

The work extends Landauer's principle to correlated quantum memories and highlights the operational role of correlations in quantum thermodynamic erasure tasks.

*Keywords: quantum Landauer erasure, assisted quantum erasure, quantum memory, thermodynamic work, quantum correlations*

- **Tags**: `memory`  `information thermodynamics`  `channels with memory`  `work extraction`  `agents`

### TL;DR

The paper develops an information-theoretic model of agents as complex self-organizing systems and analyzes the work capacity of memory channels, showing that thermodynamic efficiency requires forgetting parts of past actions.

### Motivation

The authors aim to understand how information-processing constraints shape the thermodynamics of adaptive agents, particularly the role of memory in work extraction and efficiency.

### Method

They formulate an information-theoretic model of agents and study the work capacity of channels with memory, linking memory retention and forgetting to thermodynamic performance.

### Result

The analysis shows that agents operating at thermodynamic efficiency must discard some information about their past actions, revealing a trade-off between memory and work capacity.

### Conclusion

The work provides a thermodynamics-of-agents framework connecting information flow, memory, and work extraction, with implications for the physical limits of self-organizing systems.

*Keywords: information thermodynamics, channels with memory, work extraction, agents, thermodynamic efficiency*

## 3. Reliable Entropy Estimation From Observed Statistics For Device-Independent Quantum Cryptography

- **Score**: 8.8 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-20
- **Authors**: Gereon Koßmann And René Schwonnek

- **Tags**: `certification`  `device-independent quantum cryptography`  `conditional von Neumann entropy`  `Navascués–Pironio–Acín hierarchy`  `semidefinite programming`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces an efficient numerical framework for estimating conditional von Neumann entropies directly from observed statistics in device-independent quantum cryptography.

### Motivation

Device-independent cryptography and randomness generation require reliable lower bounds on conditional von Neumann entropy under untrusted devices, using only observed statistics and quantum mechanics.

### Method

The authors develop semidefinite relaxations based on the Navascués–Pironio–Acín hierarchy, combined with an integral representation of entropy, and use projectively chosen support variables to reduce computational cost.

### Result

The method requires about half as many support variables as existing approaches and significantly reduces runtime, enabling practical randomness and security certification even in noisy conditions.

### Conclusion

This framework provides a versatile and efficient tool for device-independent quantum cryptographic protocols and integrates with modern entropy accumulation theorems.

*Keywords: device-independent quantum cryptography, conditional von Neumann entropy, Navascués–Pironio–Acín hierarchy, semidefinite programming, entropy accumulation theorem*

### TL;DR

The paper analyzes work statistics for sudden quantum quenches using random matrix theory, showing Gaussian work distributions for translation-invariant quadratic fermionic chains and characterizing non-Gaussian deviations.

### Motivation

Work statistics in non-equilibrium quantum quenches are central to quantum thermodynamics, but a general random-matrix understanding of when Gaussianity emerges or fails is lacking.

### Method

The authors map sudden-quench work distributions to traces of powers of Haar-random unitaries, represent the Loschmidt amplitude via unitary matrix models and Toeplitz determinants, and apply multivariate central limit theorems to obtain Gaussian statistics.

### Result

They derive Gaussian work distributions with explicit variance and asymptotic independence across powers, and identify conditions for non-Gaussian tails such as many interaction terms, slow decay, and Fisher-Hartwig singularities, illustrated in the XY chain with numerical diagnostics.

### Conclusion

The work provides a random-matrix framework for sudden-quench work statistics, clarifying Gaussian and non-Gaussian regimes and linking them to universality classes and singularity structure.

*Keywords: work statistics, quantum quench, random matrix theory, Loschmidt amplitude, Gaussianity*

### TL;DR

The paper develops a quantum analogue of Monge transport theory using density matrices as mass distributions, CPTP maps as transport plans, and the Jordan product 'state over time' to define a linear transport cost.

### Motivation

Classical optimal transport has been highly fruitful, motivating a principled quantum version that preserves the bilinear structure of mass distribution and transport plan.

### Method

The authors generalize mass distributions to density matrices and transport plans to completely positive trace-preserving maps, integrate them via the Jordan product into a 'state over time', and define transport cost as a linear functional of this object.

### Result

They characterize properties of the quantum transport cost and the optimal transport cost between states, and analytically compute many costs in the unitary-invariant case.

### Conclusion

The framework suggests that quantum transport cost is qualitatively different from classical Monge transport, opening a new direction in quantum information theory.

*Keywords: quantum optimal transport, states over time, completely positive trace-preserving maps, Jordan product, Monge transport*

- **Tags**: `squeezing`  `approximate quantum error correction`  `GKP codes`  `logical gate error`  `composable error`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a composable logical gate error metric for approximate quantum error correction and analyzes linear-optics gate implementations in approximate GKP codes.

### Motivation

Logical gate accuracy is difficult to quantify in approximate error correction, where physical operations cannot realize ideal logical gates and leakage out of the code space must be accounted for.

### Method

The authors define a single scalar composable logical gate error that includes target-gate deviation and code-space leakage, prove subadditivity under gate composition, and bound it using matrix elements of physical unitaries between approximate logical basis states.

### Result

For linear-optics implementations of Paulis and Cliffords in approximate GKP codes, Pauli gate error scales linearly with the squeezing parameter, while some Clifford implementations that are exact for ideal GKP codes have constant error even at infinite squeezing.

### Conclusion

The composable logical gate error provides a practical circuit-level accuracy measure for approximate QEC, and shows that ideal-GKP gate implementations do not always remain accurate in physically realizable approximate GKP codes.

*Keywords: approximate quantum error correction, GKP codes, logical gate error, composable error, linear optics*

### Result

The feedback compensates the intrinsic magnetic loss and yields strong tripartite photon-magnon-phonon coupling, which would be unattainable with the bare material parameters.

### Conclusion

Feedback-based dissipation control offers a general route to overcome material-imposed losses in hybrid quantum platforms, extending the reach of cavity magnomechanics toward coherent quantum-coherent operation and transduction.

*Keywords: cavity magnomechanics, magnon-photon-phonon coupling, feedback dissipation control, strong coupling, hybrid quantum systems*

The eigenstate thermalization hypothesis successfully predicts equilibrium expectation values and off-diagonal matrix elements, but the origin and fine structure of its Gaussian-like correlations — particularly subleading corrections and the role of symmetries — remain incompletely understood.

### Method

The authors employ free probability theory, treating matrix elements as freely independent random variables, combined with the constraints imposed by local rotational (SU(2)-like) invariance on operator overlaps in many-body eigenstates.

### Result

### Conclusion

The work sharpens ETH into a symmetry-constrained, quantitatively refined statement, showing that free probability is a natural language for capturing correlation structure beyond leading order in chaotic many-body systems.

The authors develop variational perturbation theory with a multipoint generalization, plus two pseudo-inverse-free numerical schemes: one based on a single LU decomposition and one using Krylov space recycling with preconditioned iterative methods.

### Result

### Conclusion

The approach provides a broadly applicable and efficient tool for steady-state computation and parameter exploration in open quantum systems.

Quantum graphs, introduced by Duan, Severini and Winter to capture zero-error communication over quantum channels, are continuous (non-discrete) objects, which makes it hard to build insightful examples and to compute invariants.

Quantum graphs acted on by successively smaller classical matrix groups are parametrised by triples of matrices, decomposed into a 'strange graph' classical weighted part and a purely quantum component, and analysed in diagrammatic language.

### Result

Exact formulas or bounds are obtained for the number of connected components, chromatic number, independence number and clique number, giving the first large parametric families of quantum graphs with analytically computable parameters.

### TL;DR

The paper extends Hall's exact uncertainty relation to Liouville space to analyze the geometry and probability transport underlying Krylov-space operator dynamics.

### Motivation

Krylov representations of operator dynamics contain two distinct motions—probability transport along the discrete Krylov chain and motion of the normalized amplitude vector on the associated sphere—and the authors aim to constrain these using an exact uncertainty relation.

### Method

They lift Hall's exact uncertainty relation to Liouville space and apply it to the structure of Krylov-space operator dynamics, separating chain transport from spherical geometric motion.

### Result

The lifted uncertainty relation provides bounds connecting the discrete Krylov-chain probability flow with the geometric motion of the amplitude vector on the sphere for fixed Krylov subspaces.

### Conclusion

This offers a geometric and uncertainty-based framework for understanding operator growth and Krylov complexity, with potential relevance to diagnostics of quantum chaos and operator dynamics.

*Keywords: Krylov space, operator dynamics, exact uncertainty relation, Liouville space, Krylov complexity*

## 135. Failure Of The Mean-field Hartree Approximation For A Bosonic Many-body System With non-Hermitian Hamiltonian

- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2244/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2244/)
- **Tags**: `non-Hermitian Hamiltonian`  `Hartree approximation`  `mean-field theory`  `bosonic many-body systems`  `open quantum systems`

### TL;DR

The paper proves that the mean-field Hartree approximation can fail for bosonic many-body systems governed by non-Hermitian Hamiltonians, with explicit counterexamples showing disagreement between the exact one-particle marginal and the Hartree equation.

### Motivation

Non-Hermitian Hamiltonians are used to model particle gain/loss and open-system dynamics, and the Hartree approximation underlies some quantum algorithms for nonlinear differential equations, so its validity in this setting is important to establish.

### Method

The authors analyze the general bosonic mean-field Hamiltonian with two-body interactions, derive a second time-derivative mismatch for the one-particle marginal, solve an anti-Hermitian N-qubit bosonic model analytically, and simulate a non-Hermitian Hamiltonian with real spectrum.

### Result

They show that the Hartree equation fails to capture the correct second time derivative at t=0, that the exact one-particle marginal disagrees with the Hartree solution in the thermodynamic limit, and that a finite-time transition to a mixed state can occur, absent in Hermitian systems.

### Conclusion

The findings challenge the validity of the non-Hermitian Hartree approximation for modeling gain/loss and open-system bosonic many-body dynamics, requiring additional conditions for a valid mean-field regime.

*Keywords: non-Hermitian Hamiltonian, Hartree approximation, mean-field theory, bosonic many-body systems, open quantum systems*

### TL;DR

The authors propose a rotating Kerr nonlinear resonator scheme that achieves nonreciprocal multiphoton blockade and tunable photon-number filtering by exploiting the Sagnac-Fizeau frequency shift induced by rotation.

### Motivation

Multiphoton blockade is a versatile route to nonclassical light with controllable photon statistics, and adding nonreciprocity (direction-dependent response) would enable chip-scale optical isolators and directional single-/few-photon sources; existing schemes typically lack tunability and directional control.

### Method

A Kerr nonlinear cavity is driven by frequency-matched fields in a spinning (rotating) resonator, so that clockwise and counterclockwise modes experience opposite frequency shifts; the quantum master equation is solved to compute equal-time photon correlation functions g^(n) for both propagation directions.

### Result

Rotation strength and drive detuning jointly tune the multiphoton blockade order, yielding strong nonreciprocal two-photon blockade and photon-number filtering where photon bundles of a selected number transmit only in one direction.

### Conclusion

The scheme provides a rotation-controlled knob for direction-selective nonclassical light generation and photon-number filtering, offering a route toward integrable nonreciprocal quantum photonic devices.

### TL;DR

Nonequilibrium DMFT study of how electrons and phonons in the Hubbard-Holstein model jointly thermalize after a sudden switch-on of the electron-phonon coupling.

### Motivation

Understanding whether and how coupled electronic and bosonic degrees of freedom reach a common thermal state is central to the physics of relaxation in strongly correlated materials, but a self-consistent description of both subsystems' thermalization is still lacking.

### Method

The authors combine nonequilibrium dynamical mean-field theory with a self-consistent Migdal approximation for the electron-phonon vertex and self-consistent second-order (dynamical mean-field) impurity solvers to evolve the coupled electron-phonon system in time.

### Result

The electrons and phonons are shown to thermalize self-consistently to a common temperature, with the effective electron-phonon coupling and phonon distribution readjusting during the relaxation; the coupled dynamics differs from perturbative or non-self-consistent treatments.

### Conclusion

Self-consistency in the electron-phonon self-energy is essential for correctly capturing the long-time thermalization of Hubbard-Holstein systems, providing a benchmark for approximate schemes.

### TL;DR

The paper develops a formalism for symmetry defects and gauging of finite matrix product unitary symmetries in matrix product states.

### Method

They define defect tensors for MPU symmetries that can be created, moved, and fused by local unitaries, and introduce a block-independence condition under which the defects can be promoted to gauge degrees of freedom. They also propose state-level gauging when block independence fails, including anomalous cases.

### Result

When block independence holds, gauging yields an MPS of the same bond dimension with local commuting gauge constraints. Otherwise, state-level gauging produces a locally symmetric MPS but with noncommuting gauge constraints across sites.

### Conclusion

The work generalizes symmetry-defect and gauging techniques from onsite symmetries to MPU symmetries in MPS, with implications for anomalous symmetry representations and locally symmetric tensor-network states.

*Keywords: matrix product states, matrix product unitary symmetries, gauging, symmetry defects, tensor networks*

### Result

They show efficient quantum algorithms exist for a substantially wider class of nonlinear systems and establish BQP-completeness of exponentially large nonlinear oscillator problems.

### Method

The authors analyze a tight-binding chain with uniform nearest-neighbor hopping plus a periodically rotating spin-assisted hopping term whose amplitude is weakest at the boundaries and strongest in the bulk, solving the Floquet problem for a finite chain.

### Result

The inhomogeneous drive produces a bottleneck effect (Floquet-Sambe) and localization that depends selectively on the driving frequency, with distinct behavior at the chain edges versus the bulk.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

A Floquet-based framework yields commutator-scaling error bounds for time-dependent product and multiproduct Trotter formulas, tightening gate-count estimates for digital quantum simulation of driven and adiabatic dynamics.

### Motivation

Product-formula (Trotter) decompositions are the workhorse of Hamiltonian simulation, but rigorous error bounds for time-dependent Hamiltonians, including multiproduct extrapolation schemes, have lagged behind those for static cases, leaving gate-count estimates loose.

### Method

The authors recast the stroboscopic evolution of product formulas as a Floquet problem, deriving error bounds expressed in terms of nested commutators of the Hamiltonian and its time derivatives for both product and multiproduct (Richardson-type) formulas.

### Result

The derived bounds scale with commutators rather than naive norm products, giving sharper, provably tighter gate-count and step-size estimates for simulating driven dynamics and adiabatic state preparation.

### Conclusion

The analysis provides practically useful and rigorous resource estimates for time-dependent digital quantum simulation, though it is a circuit-algorithmic error-analysis contribution rather than an information-theoretic one.

*Keywords: Trotter / product formulas, Hamiltonian simulation, Floquet analysis, multiproduct formulas, commutator error bounds*

### TL;DR

SamBa-GQW is a non-variational quantum algorithm that solves binary combinatorial optimization problems by running a continuous-time quantum walk on the solution-space graph, with hopping rates guided by classically sampled spectral information from the problem Hamiltonian.

### Motivation

Variational quantum heuristics such as QAOA and variational guided quantum walks require expensive classical outer-loop optimization, which is impractical beyond the NISQ regime; the authors seek a fully non-variational alternative that still reaches high-quality solutions.

### Method

An offline classical sampling protocol extracts information about the spectrum of the problem Hamiltonian, which is then used to define a time-dependent hopping rate for a continuous-time quantum walk over the solution-space graph, steering the walker toward low-cost vertices without any classical optimizer.

### Result

Empirical tests on MaxCut, maximum independent set, portfolio optimization, LABS, MAX-k-SAT and a quartic TSP reformulation show high-quality approximate solutions for problems up to hundreds of qubits while sampling only poly(n) states, competitive with classically optimized variational guided quantum walks and deep-circuit QAOA.

### Conclusion

SamBa-GQW is presented as a promising non-variational heuristic for combinatorial optimization that may scale to regimes beyond NISQ, though the evidence is empirical and heuristic in nature.

*Keywords: continuous-time quantum walk, combinatorial optimization, non-variational quantum algorithm, spectral sampling, QAOA comparison*

- **Tags**: `magnon`  `optical spin-orbit coupling`  `magnons`  `optical vortex`  `orbital angular momentum`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that magnons in a magnetic film can imprint orbital angular momentum onto light via spin-orbit coupling of light, generating optical vortices.

### Motivation

Optical spin-orbit coupling in spatially inhomogeneous fields is well studied, but whether collective spin excitations (magnons) can mediate transfer of angular momentum between spin and orbital degrees of freedom of light was unclear.

### Method

The authors analyze the coupling between magnon modes and structured optical fields with a broad wave-vector distribution, and derive/measure the resulting conversion of spin angular momentum into orbital angular momentum of the transmitted light.

### Result

Magnon excitation is found to generate optical vortices, i.e., light carrying well-defined orbital angular momentum, controlled by the magnon mode and the inhomogeneous wave-vector distribution.

### Conclusion

Magnon-mediated spin-orbit coupling of light provides a route to compact, tunable sources of structured light, with potential use in magnon-photon hybrid platforms.

*Keywords: optical spin-orbit coupling, magnons, optical vortex, orbital angular momentum, magnon-photon coupling*

### TL;DR

An experimental demonstration of the erratic non-Hermitian skin effect in classical phononic crystals, showing disorder-dependent localization that breaks conventional bulk-boundary correspondence.

### Motivation

The non-Hermitian skin effect challenges standard bulk-boundary correspondence, and the recently proposed 'erratic' variant arising from the interplay of disorder with locally nonreciprocal but globally reciprocal couplings has so far lacked experimental verification.

### Method

Acoustic/phononic crystal lattices are engineered with locally nonreciprocal yet globally reciprocal couplings and controlled disorder, and the resulting mode localization is mapped experimentally via spatially resolved acoustic measurements.

### Result

The experiment observes disorder-dependent, erratic localization of eigenmodes consistent with predictions for the ENHSE, confirming the breakdown of conventional bulk-boundary correspondence in this regime.

### Conclusion

The work provides the first experimental observation of the erratic non-Hermitian skin effect, establishing phononic crystals as a platform for studying disorder-driven non-Hermitian topological phenomena.

### Result

Applying a DC electric field allows a nonzero NANEs in time-reversal symmetric 2D systems with crystallographic symmetries beyond a single mirror, and the alternating-temperature-gradient scheme yields a measurable second-harmonic signal.

### Motivation

Classical branching-process descriptions of avalanche dynamics assume conservative transport and locally tree-like independence, which breaks down on real complex networks where dissipation and network structure matter.

### Method

They generalize branching-process theory to incorporate dissipation along avalanche propagation and apply it to sandpile dynamics on complex networks, comparing analytic predictions with numerics.

### Result

### Method

### Result

### TL;DR

A Li+-locked domain engineering strategy in high-entropy BNT-based ceramics breaks the temperature-stability bottleneck of thermal-detection energy storage, reaching Wrec = 11.43 J cm^-3 with η = 84.67% and Δη/η < 0.3% up to 320 °C.

### Method

### Result

The ceramics achieve a recoverable energy density of 11.43 J cm^-3, an efficiency of 84.67%, and efficiency variation below 0.3% up to 320 °C, while also providing self-diagnostic thermometry.

### Conclusion

Li+-locked domain engineering enables thermally stable high-entropy energy-storage ceramics suited to harsh-environment and thermal-detection applications, though the work lies outside quantum information science.

- **Tags**: `electronegative plasma`  `ion-acoustic waves`  `Schamel trapped-particle distribution`  `nonlinear Schrödinger equation`  `modulational instability`

### TL;DR

This paper derives and analyzes a generalized Schamel-type nonlinear Schrödinger equation governing amplitude modulation of ion-acoustic wave packets in an unmagnetized electronegative plasma with trapped negative ions.

### Motivation

Electronegative plasmas containing trapped negative ions are relevant to weakly collisional laboratory and space environments, and the authors aim to understand how trapped-particle distributions modify the nonlinear envelope dynamics of ion-acoustic wave packets.

### Method

A reductive perturbation (multiple-scales) expansion is applied to the fluid-Poisson system with cold positive ions, Boltzmann electrons, and a Schamel trapped-particle distribution for negative ions, yielding a generalized nonlinear Schrödinger equation whose coefficients depend on the trapping parameter.

### Result

The generalized Schamel nonlinear Schrödinger equation admits both bright- and dark-type envelope solutions, and the trapping parameter is shown to modulate the dispersion, nonlinearity, and stability properties of these wave packets.

### Conclusion

Trapped negative ions qualitatively reshape the modulational dynamics of ion-acoustic wave packets in electronegative plasmas, providing a theoretical basis for interpreting nonlinear electrostatic structures in such media.

*Keywords: electronegative plasma, ion-acoustic waves, Schamel trapped-particle distribution, nonlinear Schrödinger equation, modulational instability*

## 408. Nonflow Subtraction Beyond Two-particle Correlations

- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/aea707](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/aea707)
- **Tags**: `correlations`  `nonflow subtraction`  `collective flow`  `multi-particle cumulants`  `small collision systems`

### TL;DR

The paper develops a general framework for subtracting nonflow contributions from multi-particle flow cumulants in small collision systems, using HIJING as a nonflow-only testbed.

### Motivation

Establishing collective flow in small systems is a prerequisite for determining minimal conditions for quark-gluon plasma formation, but multi-particle collectivity has been unverified at low multiplicity because residual nonflow in higher-order cumulants was never quantified.

### Method

The authors build a nonflow subtraction framework for n-particle cumulants based on approximate scaling of nonflow in the independent-source picture, using correlators containing k=2 as nonflow-dominated estimators and testing against three target observables in O+O and p+Au collisions with HIJING simulations.

### Result

Most nonflow is removed, with residual fractions typically 20%-30% at the two-particle level, and the best estimator varies across targets; a previously overlooked multiplicity-reweighting correction explains the long-standing undersubtraction of naive 1/N-scaling and grows with correlator order.

### Conclusion

The framework provides a systematic route to nonflow subtraction beyond two-particle correlations, broadening the class of multi-particle observables accessible to small-system flow studies.

*Keywords: nonflow subtraction, collective flow, multi-particle cumulants, small collision systems, quark-gluon plasma*

- **Tags**: `bidirectional pedestrian flow`  `angled corridors`  `flow ratio`  `crowd dynamics`  `empirical experiment`

### TL;DR

A controlled laboratory experiment characterizes bidirectional pedestrian flow through corridors with six different bend angles and four flow ratios.

### Motivation

Designers of pedestrian facilities and crowd managers need empirical data on how corridor geometry and opposing flow ratios affect walking dynamics, an aspect that remains poorly quantified for angled corridors.

### Method

Experiments with 48 participants were run in corridors spanning six angles (from a specified range) and four directional flow ratios, with trajectories analyzed to extract flow, density, and lane-formation statistics.

### Result

The study quantifies how corridor angle and flow ratio systematically alter bidirectional flow efficiency, lane formation, and congestion patterns.

### Conclusion

The findings provide empirically grounded guidance for pedestrian facility design and crowd-management strategies in angled corridors.

*Keywords: bidirectional pedestrian flow, angled corridors, flow ratio, crowd dynamics, empirical experiment*
